<insert>A</insert>

This page features a circular emblem printed on an inset sheet, serving as a symbolic visual introduction to the manuscript. The central image is contained within an oval vignette: at the top, a golden sun radiates rays of light downward onto a white pelican. The bird is depicted in the "Pelican in her Piety" <term>Pelican in her Piety: A traditional Christian and alchemical symbol of self-sacrifice, where a mother bird wounds her own breast to feed her chicks with her blood; in alchemy, this represents the "multiplication" or spiritual refinement of the work.</term> motif, nourishing three small chicks with its own blood. 

The pelican and its nest are positioned upon a silver crescent moon. Below this moon sits a white, cubical altar or pedestal, marked with a red saltire <note>a diagonal, X-shaped cross</note> and four red roses, which are hallmark symbols of <term>Rosicrucianism: A mystical movement that emerged in the early 17th century, combining elements of alchemy, Kabbalah, and Christian mysticism.</term> This entire central scene is framed by a green border and an outer ring containing the title of the collection:

->HERMETIC PHILOSOPHY<-
<note>original: "PHILOSOPHIA HERMETICA"</note>

<summary>This frontispiece introduces a collection of Hermetic writings through a symbolic emblem featuring a "Pelican in her Piety" and Rosicrucian imagery. The illustration represents the alchemical and spiritual themes of sacrifice, purification, and the union of solar and lunar forces.</summary>

### The Work of the Laboratory

The following terms appear around a boiling pot and furnace, representing the primary stages of the alchemical process:

->Dissolve<-
<note>original Latin: "Solve"</note>

->Congeal<-
<note>original Latin: "Coagula"; the axiom "Solve et Coagula" is the foundational command of alchemy, instructing the practitioner to first break down a substance to its base components and then reunite them into a higher, purified state.</note>

->Shape<-
<note>original Latin: "Finge"; meaning to fashion or mold the resulting substance into its final form.</note>

---

On the right side of the furnace, three glass flasks are stacked, representing the <term>Tria Prima: The three "prime" components—salt, sulfur, and mercury—which alchemists believed composed all material things, here personified as the components of a living being.</term>

->One in all<-
->All in one.<-
<note>original German: "Eins in allen / Alles in eins." This is a central tenet of Hermeticism, suggesting that the entire universe (the macrocosm) is reflected within a single object or person (the microcosm).</note>

### The Solar Essence

Within the large ornate border at the bottom of the page, a poetic fragment links humanity to the celestial forces:

->The Breath<-
->of the Sun<-
->the Essence<-
->Adam has<-
<note>The original German "Der Ath / der Son / das Wes / hat Ada" likely completes as: "Der Athem der Sonnen, das Wesen hat Adam." This implies that the first man, Adam—and by extension, the alchemist—carries the vital, living breath of the sun within his own essence, serving as the link between heaven and earth.</note>

<summary>This illustration depicts the synthesis of laboratory chemistry and spiritual philosophy. It shows how the physical processes of "dissolving and congealing" are used to refine the three parts of the human essence (Body, Spirit, and Soul) into a unified whole.</summary>

<meta>This page is the beginning of the preface (Vorrede). The signature mark ")* ( 2" indicates this is the second leaf of the introductory section. The catchword "occu" at the bottom right suggests the next page begins with a word such as "occupierte" (occupied/engaged).</meta>

# Preface

->To the kind reader.<-

Kind, dear reader! It is credibly told of Anthony the Hermit <note>original: "Eremita Anthonio"; referring to Saint Anthony the Great (c. 251–356 AD), a prominent Desert Father and key figure in early Christian monasticism</note>, that while he was staying in a wilderness to serve God without hindrance, he came upon a clear, rushing little brook; and as he stood enthralled by its loveliness, he [occupied] himself for a somewhat longer time through the contemplation of the wonderful order of God in His creations <note>original: "Geschöpfen"; can refer to both living creatures and the broader natural world</note>...

<summary>This page opens the preface with a moralizing anecdote about Saint Anthony the Great contemplating God's divine order while observing a stream in the wilderness.</summary>
<keywords>preface, Anthony the Hermit, Saint Anthony, divine order, wilderness, contemplation, nature</keywords>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page continues the anecdote from the previous leaf, completing the sentence regarding Saint Anthony's contemplation. The text transitions from the specific story of the hermit to a broader philosophical application regarding the study of nature as a divine work.</meta>

### Preface

[occupied], he finally resolved within himself that he wished to seek out the origin of this little brook, so as to sharpen his thoughts through the meditation he had begun. He therefore traveled ever onward against the current, until at last he found a beautiful spring gushing forth from a rock; he drank from it and gave thanks to the Creator, his God, for it.

Whether this be a true history or an invented encouragement to stir human lethargy <note>original: "Trägheit"; refers here to intellectual or spiritual sloth that prevents one from investigating the natural world</note> into investigating the works of God, it is nonetheless a simple and yet necessary instruction for a beginning—or even a well-practiced—philosopher <note>term: philosopher; in this context, the term refers to a "natural philosopher," someone who seeks to understand the physical world as a way of understanding the divine</note> who wishes to devote himself, as David did <note>referring to the biblical King David, who in the Psalms often writes of praising God through the observation of the heavens and the earth</note>, to contemplating the works of the Lord's hands. For if anyone from the land

<meta>catchword: Havilah (Hevila)</meta>

<summary>This page concludes the story of Saint Anthony finding the source of a stream and applies it as a metaphor for the "philosopher" who seeks the divine origin of all natural things. It emphasizes the study of nature as a spiritual duty aligned with the Psalms of David.</summary>
<keywords>contemplation, Saint Anthony, natural philosophy, creation, King David, meditation, lethargy, divine works</keywords>

So also, if one could travel from the <term>circumferences</term> <note>original: "circumferentiis"; the outer boundaries or shells of physical form</note> and outermost rinds of the elements into their innermost essence: from the earth into its center; from the water to the <term>liquor of nature and life</term> <note>original: "liquori naturae et vitae"; the essential animating fluid or "life-water" that sustains the natural world</note>; from the air into the <term>nursery of powers</term> <note>original: "virtutum Seminarium"; literally a 'seedbed of virtues,' referring to the air as a medium containing the generative seeds or forces of all things</note>, or the seminal propagation; and from the fire into its unburnable substance—then he would behold with the highest amazement the composition of the future new heaven and

# Preface

<meta>catchword: If</meta>

Earth, of which Saint Peter speaks in his second epistle, chapter 3, might be understood. When one thus goes back from the division <note>The "division" refers to the separation of the primordial unity into the four elements (Earth, Water, Air, Fire)</note> to the first single creature—which Peter in the aforementioned place calls "a water," and the **philosophers** <note>In this context, "philosophers" refers to alchemists or natural philosophers seeking the fundamental truths of nature</note> call **Chaos** or **chaotic water** <note>original: "Chao oder Chaotica aqua"</note>—one finds that in this single [source] all things lie **implicitly** <note>original: "implicite"; enfolded or potentially present within a single substance before they are manifest</note> and are to be found. Conversely, all **unfolded things** <note>original: "res explicatae"; physical objects and beings that have emerged or manifested in the visible world</note> can again be brought back into one.

And in this consists—since all creatures, **according to the internal being of the first Creation** <note>original: "secundum internum esse primae Creationis"</note>, are all **of the same kind** <note>original: "Homogenea"; sharing the same essential substance</note>, because they had one mother and a general origin before the separation—the **symbolization, concordance, and harmony** of all things. To experienced philosophers, this is so lovely, graceful, and wonderful that it far, far surpasses **vocal, instrumental, and both types of music joined together** <note>original: "vocalem, instrumentalem et utramque conjunctam musicam"</note>.

<summary>The author explains that all created things share a "homogeneous" origin in a primordial "Chaotic Water," arguing that everything in the universe can be traced back to this single source. He claims that the resulting harmony of nature is more beautiful than any man-made music.</summary>
<keywords>primordial chaos, first water, Saint Peter, implicit vs explicit, harmony of nature, alchemy, homogeneity, cosmic music</keywords>

Here begins the abyss <note>original: "Abgrund"; a term often used in mystical theology to describe the infinite, unfathomable depth of the Divine essence that exists before all created things</note> or infinite depth of God alone, from whom everything has begun, and in whom everything must also end. In such a manner it is also no less to be considered how all creatures have been brought from the internal into the external, from the core <note>original: "Kern"</note> into the rind <note>original: "Rinde"; the outer shell or physical manifestation</note>, from the subtle into the coarse, and from the spirit into the body. If one now [wishes to ... <note>The sentence continues onto the next page</note>] the inner, pure spiritual core

# Preface

and essence, then there belongs to this the Art of Separation <term>Scheidekunst: an early term for chemistry or alchemy, literally the "art of parting" or "separating" substances</term>, commonly called Alchemy, which produces its natural application in the elements and other specified creatures. It does this by having separated the subtle parts of the earth into greening, growing fruits and animals, and the coarse parts into seeds and stones; the subtle of the water into fish and weaving <note>original: "webenden"; a term used in the Luther Bible for the "swarming" or "moving" life within the waters</note> animals, and the coarse into roaring and strong streams; the subtle of the air into birds and flying creatures, and the coarse into a rushing wind; the subtle of the fire into shining planets and stars, and the coarse into a firmament <note>original: "Veste"; the fixed vault of heaven or the physical shell of the cosmos</note>. All of this was previously separated out of a Chaos <note>original: "Chao"; the primordial, unformed state of matter</note> or water through the Word. This Art of Separation is so powerfully implanted in man that through it he [receives] his body’s sustenance, strength

It is known to <term>theologians <note>original: "Theologis"</note></term> that man—born of sinful seed and coarse <term>Adamic <note>original: "adamischen"; referring to the fallen, earthly state of humanity inherited from Adam according to Christian tradition</note></term> flesh and blood—cannot possess the Kingdom of Heaven and the holy place, unless such a body, with all its lusts and desires, dies away completely, and the seed of that same body, or its inner essence, is moved again through a holy baptism into the blood of Christ, and thereby <term>coagulated <note>original: "coagulirt"; an alchemical term for the process of solidifying a substance</note></term>, <term>fixed <note>original: "figirt"; an alchemical term meaning to make a volatile or spiritual substance stable and permanent</note></term>, and made permanent for all eternity into a new heavenly and immortal body. Therefore, if you wish to be a lover and diligent seeker of this art <term>This Art: Alchemy, often called the "Royal Art," presented here as a sacred science of both matter and spirit</term>, know that this art was given to our first father Adam by Almighty God in Paradise <note>original: *Paradeiß*</note> after his creation. ## A Warning and Instruction for all lovers of this art, in which they may mirror and behold themselves, regarding what is essential and used in the foundation of the work, and whether it be possible or not to achieve the true art, and how it is constituted.

Here I now wish to have faithfully warned everyone, and especially my dear children, without any deceit, against other fantasies <note>original: "Phantaseyen," referring to the delusional or impractical theories of false alchemists</note>, and I will faithfully instruct and point the way in these things with the help of God.

Firstly, I wish to have faithfully requested and warned you that you especially guard yourselves against the "alchemy-trickery" <term>Allchimisterey: A derogatory term used by true practitioners to distinguish their "Sacred Art" from the fraudulent or clumsy "puffers" who only sought to make gold through trickery</term> of the common rabble; for I have experienced so much that I will advise no one to enter into it, or to lay his hand upon it, for the reason that this art is so hidden that no person on earth may find it, unless it be the case that Sun and Moon <note>In alchemical symbolism, the Sun represents Gold or the masculine principle, and the Moon represents Silver or the feminine principle. Their "coming together" signifies the chemical wedding or conjunction necessary for the Great Work</note> come together. Dear [Children,]

### A Warning and Instruction for
### all lovers of this art, wherein
### they may mirror and behold themselves,
### regarding what is used and essential
### in the foundation of the work, whether
### it is possible or not to attain
### the true art, and how its
### form is constituted.

Herein I now wish to have faithfully warned everyone, and especially my dear children <note>original: "Kinder." In alchemical literature, "children" or "sons of the art" refers to dedicated students and initiates of the hermetic secrets</note>, without any deceit, against other fantasies; and with the help of God, I will faithfully instruct and show these things.

First, I want to have faithfully requested and warned you that you especially guard yourselves against the alchemy of the common rabble <note>A reference to "puffers"—those who sought only to make gold through literal, clumsy chemistry without spiritual or theoretical understanding</note>, for I have experienced so much that I will advise no one to enter into it, or to lay his hand upon it, for the reason that this art is so hidden that no person on earth may find it, unless it be the case that Sun and Moon come together <note>In alchemical symbolism, the "Sun" and "Moon" represent Gold and Silver, or the masculine (Sulfur) and feminine (Mercury) principles. Their union—the "Chymical Wedding"—is the central requirement for creating the Philosopher's Stone</note>. Dear

<summary>The author concludes a poetic recipe for an elixir and begins a stern warning to students, distinguishing "true art" from the fraudulent "alchemy of the rabble." He emphasizes that the secret is divinely hidden and requires the symbolic union of the Sun and Moon.</summary>

## Another Understanding of our Blessed Stone.

The philosopher <term>Morienus</term> <note>Morienus Romanus was a legendary 7th-century alchemist, traditionally cited as the teacher who brought alchemical secrets from the East to the West.</note> speaks and calls our **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the ultimate goal of alchemy, capable of transmutation and healing.</note> a "water," in which he writes no lies. O you greatest of all natures, who assembles the purification. O you nature most base <note>Original: "schlimm." In this context, it refers to the "vile" or common starting material of the work which is eventually exalted.</note>, who exalts nature; you are crowned and born with a light, and you contain the four elements within yourself, and the **fifth essence** <note>Also known as the Quintessence; the pure, celestial essence that exists beyond the four physical elements (earth, air, fire, water).</note> has sprung from you; of this the simple-minded rejoice in their simplicity, that nature has received the <term>composition</term> <note>Original: "Zuhauffsetzung." Literally a "heaping together" or a joining of parts into a whole.</note> before the composition, unknown to human foresight; although you yourself give birth to the mist, and you are indeed a mother, you require no help of any kind; therefore nature contains nature, so that nature is not separated from the work of nature, and it is a simple thing, an easy understanding.

## Of the Treasure and Tincture.

Likewise, once you have worked this far and attained the Red <note>The "Red" refers to the *Rubedo*, the final stage of the alchemical "Great Work," signaling the completion of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, you should know that you have a treasure that the whole world cannot repay nor afford; for this transforms all metals into true gold, which is much better than that which the earth has made through the action of the sun, and in medicine, it surpasses all gold. For it makes all fleshly <term>bodies</term> <note>original: "Corpora." In this context, it refers to the human body as a physical vessel.</note> healthy, if one uses a poppy seed's worth <note>original: "Mohnkörnlein." A tiny unit of measurement common in early modern pharmaceutical texts.</note> of this tincture in wine, and its great virtues cannot all be told. 

If you now wish to have a tincture for <term>Luna</term> <note>original: "Luna." The alchemical name for silver, associated with the moon.</note>, take 5 <term>Loth</term> <note>A traditional German unit of weight, roughly equal to 15 grams or half an ounce.</note> of the white substance, and cast it upon 500 Loth of Luna that is finely refined; when it is melted, the same will altogether become a tincture and medicine. Of this, you then take 1 Loth and cast it upon 500 Loth of <term>Venus</term> <note>original: "Venus." The alchemical name for copper.</note> or other metals, and it transforms into true Luna. 

But for the Red, which you have worked to such a high degree, take 1 part to 1000 parts of <term>Sol</term> <note>original: "Solis." The alchemical name for gold, associated with the sun.</note>, and this will become a medicinal tincture; of that same substance, cast again 1 part onto 1000 parts of Venus or other metals, and it will be transformed into true Sol. You need not buy any Sol or Luna for this, for the first addition you may well do with a...

You should also know that no more than two things belong to this <term>Art</term> <note>The "Art" (German: *Kunst*) is a standard historical term for Alchemy.</note>: the one is Body, the other is Spirit. The one is fixed—that is, stable—the other is volatile or unstable. These must now be transformed, so that the fixed must become volatile, and the volatile must become fixed. That is to say: the <term>body</term> <note>original: "Corpus."</note> becomes water, and the water becomes body, which then becomes water within itself again. These two must be joined together—the dry with the moist—so that they may never be separated. And if they are to be joined together, they must come from a single thing, for no thing accepts anything into itself except that which is of its own nature.

It is the same in our Art: there is a <term>Matter</term> <note>original: "Materia."</note> composed by Nature and not by human hands. That same matter is divided; it stands as the <term>Eagle</term> <note>original: "Adler." In alchemical symbolism, the "Eagle" often represents the volatile spirit or the process of sublimation (rising upward).</note>, which is the water that has been drawn off. Thus the body remains dead and destroyed. If it is to live now, its <term>Spirit</term> <note>original: "Spiritus."</note> must be given back to it, piece by piece, just as you see that it swallows the eagles one after another again. And when it becomes living once more, it loses all its impurity and takes on a pure, new body. Then the body and spirit shall never die again but remain forever fixed, just as body and soul shall do on the Day of Judgment.

## Another Understanding of our Stone.

It is a <term>Soap of the Wise</term> <note>original: "Seifen der Weisen." In alchemy, this refers to a substance—often a prepared salt—that "cleanses" the base metals of their impurities, much like common soap cleanses the body.</note>, it is a salt and a root of the whole Art and the key thereto, and that which opens and shuts again, which no man can either open or shut without that same key; and there is no one in this world who might come to a perfect understanding unless he knows the salt and knows its preparation, which takes place in a suitable place that is moist and warm, and where its moisture may dissolve within, so its substance remains perfect. Herewith ends this saying of <term>Geber</term> <note>Geber is the Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan (c. 721–813), a foundational figure in alchemy whose writings heavily influenced European hermetic thought.</note>.

### Interpretation.

You should know that this is a salt, as this philosopher Geber says, although in the beginning it has not the form of a salt, yet it is called a salt and is indeed salt, alike black and stinking; for in the work it becomes like <term>clotted blood</term> <note>original: "geronnen Blut." This refers to a specific color and consistency change during the heating of the alchemical "matter" in the flask.</note>, and through long duration it becomes white, pure, refined, and clear, and this is a good, noble salt, and through itself it becomes impure and then pure again. It <term>dissolves</term> <note>original: "solvirt." From the Latin *solve*.</note> and <term>coagulates</term> <note>original: "coagulirt." From the Latin *coagula*.</note> itself; that is, as the philosopher says: it opens and shuts itself. In that it coagulates, it shuts the Art, and in that it dissolves, it opens

## From Senior: Another Insight into Matter and Form

The <term>Philosopher</term> <note>original: "Philosophus." This likely refers to Senior Zadith (Zadith ben Hamuel), a 10th-century alchemist whose "Table of Cebes" was highly influential in European alchemy.</note> speaks thus: natural things are those born and produced through Nature from a natural origin—**Matter** and **Form**. Just as a stone or a tree consists of **Matter** and **Form** through Nature, so too is the "natural" defined by what is composed of these two. Regarding the nature of our **Stone**, in its first part, it is <term>Lunaria</term> <note>original: "Lunaria," literally "moon-plant," often signifying the white or silver stage of the work, or a specific botanical/mineral secret.</note> derived powerfully from a white, coagulated earth. As the Philosopher says: "Place our <term>Sun</term> <note>original: "Solem," referring to the alchemical gold or the masculine, solar principle.</note> into our white, foliated earth." 

However, the instrument with which our Art is composed is water <term>rectified</term> <note>distilled and purified multiple times.</note> two or three times through the <term>B. M.</term> <note>original: "Balneum Mariae" or "Mary’s Bath." A water bath used for gentle, indirect heating, much like a modern bain-marie.</note>. This water is a vapor of a fatty nature and the substance closest to our Stone. Within this richness, a substance is hidden; it is a penetrating moisture and the ultimate consolation for the human body. With this water, one must [extract] from the metals their inner **Mer-**

<meta>catchword: curium (completing the word "Mercurium" or Mercury on the following page).</meta>

## The Purification of the Earth from its Superfluous Earthiness

This aforementioned earth or <term>Matter</term> <note>original: "Materia."</note> you shall make small, rub, or <term>calcine</term> <note>original: "calciniren." The process of heating a substance to high temperatures to drive off volatile aspects and reduce it to a powder.</note> so that you receive its water and spirit. Then put it into a glass and pour over it a simple <term>Water of Life</term> <note>original: "Aqua Vitae." Literally "Water of Life," usually referring to concentrated alcohol or a powerful solvent used for extraction.</note> so that it floats three or four fingers' breadth above the matter. Set it for an hour in the heat and filter it. Afterward, distilled by means of a <term>bath</term> <note>original: "balneum." A heat-transfer method, likely a water bath, to ensure gentle and even heating.</note>, you shall calcine the <term>dregs</term> <note>original: "Feces." The solid waste or sediment remaining after distillation.</note> again and extract them with their water as you did before, as long as you find nothing more in the earth. You should preserve this until the next stage of the work. All these waters thus extracted and preserved, you shall distill through a <term>water bath</term> <note>original: "M. B." Abbreviation for Maria’s Bath (Balneum Mariae), a gentle double-boiler method.</note> with gentle fire. And at the bottom of the <term>gourd-flask</term> <note>original: "Cucurbit." The lower part of a distillation apparatus, often shaped like a gourd.</note> you shall find a beautiful matter, like a <term>crystal stone</term> <note>original: "Crystallenstein."</note>, purified of all earthiness, which we call our earth. and form, excluding the noble Godhead <note>original: "Gottheit"</note>, which no thing can equal; just as, according to humanity and nature, Christ was reckoned the very worst of this world and was cast out and rejected by the people of Israel, yet He was so good that heaven and earth could not repay Him.

In just the same way, it is also with our **Stone** <term>The Philosopher's Stone; the legendary substance capable of transmuting metals and perfecting life</term> in this world among natural things; for the well in which our Stone is found is called the **Well of Natures** <note>original: "Brunn der Naturen"; a common alchemical metaphor for the source of the "prime matter" or the base substance used to start the Work.</note>. For just as all growing things are ripened <note>original: "gezieglet"; literally "tiled" or "fired," referring here to the hardening or maturing process under the sun's heat.</note> by nature through the heat of the **sun**, so our Stone is born through nature, once it has been ripened. 

Now take this water, in which our Stone is contained—just as you find it, as it is, and not otherwise; take nothing from it, and add nothing to it, for you must prepare it with its own nature. Then put the water into a **flask** <term>Kolben; a round glass vessel with a long neck used for distillation</term>, and separate and draw the moist from the dry; thus the **body** <term>Corpus; the solid, physical matter that remains in the vessel after the liquid has been evaporated</term> remains alone in the glass, and the water passes over into the **receiving vessel** <term>Vorlag; the container that collects the liquid or "spirit" at the end of a distillation setup</term>. 

Join these together again, as you know how, and then you have done its work. Know that the water, in which our Stone is contained, is harmoniously tempered to the **four elements** <note>Earth, Air, Fire, and Water; the basic building blocks of matter in medieval science.</note>; for you will find Earth, and Oil, and Water in the work—that is, **Body, Soul, and Spirit** <note>The "Alchemical Trinity" representing the three parts of all created matter.</note>. As the Earth at the bottom is the Body, the Oil staying with the Earth is the Soul, and the Water that is distilled from it is the Spirit. Therein you find two primary—

Note that what the earth lacks, heaven can give in abundance; yet that which it gives us here is not from nature <note>The author suggests the alchemical gift is of a supernatural or celestial origin, rather than a common product of the physical world</note>. And yet, our Stone is not of all these natures, acting just as the imperishable heaven does, for all power is imprinted in them by the Master through a divine art, and also with the help of fire, through which the power is imprinted upon it and has embraced it; thank God always.

<meta>This page transitions from the conclusion of the 1526 epistle to a theoretical discussion of the "Stone." # The Sun is its father, the Moon is its mother.

When these two **spirits** <term>Geister; the volatile or gaseous components of a substance</term> are present, they give birth to a **Stone** <term>The Philosopher's Stone</term>. The Sun <note>original: "Sol"</note> is the **Sulfur** <term>Sulphur; the active, combustible, masculine principle in alchemy</term>, and the Moon <note>original: "Luna"</note> is **Mercury** <term>Mercurius; the passive, fluid, feminine principle</term>. From these, a Philosopher’s Stone is made. Make it from one part Sulfur and four parts Mercury. This Sulfur dries out its soul, so one holds the other: hot, dry Sulfur, and cold, moist Mercury. Mark and understand it rightly: just as that which was previously water dissolves <note>original: "solvirt"; to break down or liquefy a solid</note> back into water, so too the **body** <note>original: "Leichnam," literally "corpse"; the solid matter remaining after the spirit has been extracted</note> dissolves into Mercury, if it was Mercury before.

## Concerning the first matter, or the seed of metals, which is enclosed in every seed; there are two, that is, man and woman.

Metals are things that grow, and they are also seeds of the earth, just as are all the seeds of herbs. Therefore, they must also be moved within their earth, in which they generate <note>original: "generiren"; to produce, give life, or procreate</note> and bring forth fruit, like other growing things. If that were not to happen, no metal would have grown, nor would the seed be in the metal that is smelted from the earth. And when the seed is brought into its own soil, it has just as well

## A Beautiful Instruction on the True Art.

The Philosophers say: no dissolution of the body occurs without the **coagulation** <term>Coagulirung; the process of a liquid or spirit becoming solid or "fixed"</term> of the spirit. As soon as the **spirit** <note>original: "Spiritus"</note> is transformed into the **body** <note>original: "Corpus"</note>, it possesses its power. You should know that as long as the spirit is still volatile <note>original: "flüchtig"; meaning it evaporates or escapes when heated</note> and not fixed, it cannot work. However, as soon as it becomes fixed, it accomplishes everything it should in an instant. Therefore, one must deal with it as the baker <note>original: "Beck"</note> does with bread: take a little of the spirit and give it to the body, just as the baker adds the leaven <note>original: "Sauerteig"; sourdough starter or yeast</note> to the flour; then the leaven transforms the entire substance into leaven. Our spirit does exactly the same,

*Hyle* <note>original: "Hyle"; a Greek term used by alchemists to refer to "prime matter," the fundamental substance of the universe</note> is the first *matter*, and the <term>Salt of the Philosophers</term> <note>original: "Salz der Philosophorum"</note>, the <term>Azoth</term> <note>the "universal medicine" or essential mercurial spirit</note> and seed of all metals, which is drawn from the body of <term>Magnesia</term> and <term>Lunaria</term> <note>referring here to symbolic alchemical bodies—the "mother" substance and the "moon-like" or silver-related essence—rather than modern minerals or plants</note>.

## Now follows the second part of this book.

Firstly, it should be known that when God the Almighty in the beginning [created] all heavenly and earthly bodies, and placed our first father Adam into Paradise, and had forbidden him from eating the fruit of the tree—the apple—upon [penalty of] eternal death; at that same time, before Adam transgressed the commandment, man had no need for any earthly thing, considering that man at this time was still immortal. 

But as soon as he had fallen by eating the forbidden fruit of the <term>Knowledge of Good and Evil</term> <note>original: "Erkanntniß Gutes und Böses"</note>, he immediately became needy of all things in the world. After he was brought out of Paradise into misery—that is, into this sorrowful world—by the angel at God's command, he brought away nothing other than poverty, plague, sickness, tribulation, anxiety, and need, and finally also bitter death. 

However, if he had not transgressed God’s commandment, he would have lived his time, such as 2000 years <note>The author refers to a common contemporary belief that man's natural lifespan before the Fall or in a state of grace was significantly longer than the current human age</note>, in Paradise, and then would have been taken from here up toward Heaven; and every human of his lineage likewise. But because he was disobedient, God placed His displeasure upon him and his descendants, together with the entire earthly ground—

...and lies, held under a single proverb: truth for their children and disciples, and lies for the foolish, the ignorant, and the unwise, who interpret the lies as truth and vice versa <note>original: "& é contra"</note>. For the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: "Philosophi"; in this context, the "Philosophers of Nature" or masters of alchemy</note> have never been found to lie. Why? Because they understood them differently, they took incorrect materials: where they should have taken <term>active waters</term> <note>original: "würkende Wasser"; these are the mercurial solvents or acids necessary to break down the "body" of a metal</note>, they took rocks and flints, sulfur, salt, metal, and various substances in which there is no power. <term>Fifth Element (Quintum Elementum)</term>: The purest part of a substance, believed to be the life force that animates the four physical elements.

### Now I will teach you how you shall make the fire and the water, wherein *Mercury* is prepared or fixed, into a true tincture, white or red.

You shall take the unslaked fire or lime <note>original: *Kalch*; quicklime or a caustic alchemical substance that generates heat when moistened</note>, of which the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: *Philosophi*; the ancient alchemical authorities</note> speak, which grows upon the trees, wherein God himself burns with divine love. Understand [by this] not the common *Mercury* that you would wish to fix into a water or fire, but rather the *Mercury* that is hidden in this aforementioned water or fire; it is fixed within itself. 

Likewise, the natural master speaks of the fire for this Art: one shall putrefy <note>original: *putrificiren*; to break down or rot the material to its base state</note>, clarify <note>original: *clarificiren*; to purify</note>, coagulate <note>original: *coaguliren*; to thicken or solidify</note>, and fix <note>original: *figiren*; to make stable against heat so it no longer evaporates</note> the *Mercury* in an unquenchable fire, wherein God himself burns, but with the Sun <note>the alchemical symbol for gold and spiritual perfection</note> in divine love for the comfort of all mankind. Without this fire, the Art can nevermore be accomplished. 

This is the Fire of the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: *Feuer der Philosophorum*</note> which they have hidden and concealed when they write, so that no one may understand their words. Because of this, many fall into error, and many a man dies in poverty because they do not recognize the Fire of the <term>Philosophers</term>. ...united with the <term>Sun</term> <note>original: *Sole*; Latin for Sun, referring here to the alchemical principle of Gold</note>, so that they all three become one thing and no one shall separate them.

Likewise, the holy teachers say that just as God the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit—the Holy <term>Trinity</term> <note>original: *Dreyfaltigkeit*</note>—are united in these three persons and yet remain one single true God; so too does the fire unite these three things: <term>Body</term> <note>original: *Corpus*</note>, <term>Spirit</term> <note>original: *Spiritus*</note>, and <term>Soul</term> <note>original: *Anima*</note>—that is, the <term>Sun</term> <note>Gold</note>, <term>Mercury</term> <note>Quicksilver</note>, and the Soul. Likewise, the fire nourishes and distributes the soul—that is, the metal <note>In alchemical thought, the 'Soul' often refers to the oily, sulfurous principle that gives a metal its color and allows it to join with others.</note> that joins the two together, so that all three become one and remain together eternally, in the same way as the fire unites God, Man, and Blood. And just as fire multiplies and burns throughout the whole world, so truly does this <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: *Tinctur*; the transformative agent or Philosopher’s Stone</note> multiply, and this fire shall enter into all metals, so that one part transforms 200 parts, in the third [multiplication] 300, and in the fourth 400, into the most noble metals.

Likewise, the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>the ancient alchemical authorities</note> call this fire the "Fire of the Holy Spirit" with such dignity, for just as fire in God was transformed into true flesh and blood and remains inseparable eternally, so truly in this fire are the <term>Sun</term> <note>Gold</note>, <term>Moon</term> <note>original: *Luna*; Silver</note>, and <term>Mercury</term> transformed into a true <term>Tincture</term>. This remains steadfast through all tests <note>original: *Proben*; metallurgical assays used to prove the purity of a metal</note>, which lasts and shall last forever, and it transmuteth <note>original: *tingiren*; from the Latin *tingere*, meaning to dye or fundamentally change the color and nature of a substance</note> especially within <term>Mercury</term>. Just as God feeds many a great sinner with His holy flesh and blood, so the <term>Tincture</term> <term>transmutes</term> all coarse metals.

<summary>This page uses the theological mystery of the Holy Trinity as a direct analogy for the alchemical union of Body, Spirit, and Soul (represented by Gold, Mercury, and the metallic Soul). It describes the resulting "Tincture" as an infinitely multiplying substance capable of transforming base metals into noble ones, surviving all physical tests.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "three things" or "manual things" mentioned at the end of page 71. ### Now I also wish to teach how the
### three things work in the Art, and
### I will begin with all three
### at once.

The wise master Plato says that every farmer who wishes to sow grain must first have a good field that is well-plowed and manured; the field must be quite good and cleared of all weeds before the grain is sown into it. After that, the grain must also be pure before it is sown into the field.

Likewise, for this, the farmer must also have moisture through rain, from which the grain decays and becomes living again; afterward, he must have fire—that is, the warmth of the sun—by which the grain becomes timely and ripe. In exactly the same way must one proceed with this <term>Art</term> <note>original: *Kunst*; the Great Work of alchemy</note> and not otherwise. Namely, first prepare your things, or the seed that you wish to sow—that is, your <term>Matter</term> <note>original: *Materie*; the base substance or "body" used in the experiment</note>—which you shall make pure as I have taught you, and as the following <term>Turba</term> <note>referring to the *Turba Philosophorum* (Assembly of the Philosophers), one of the most famous and ancient alchemical texts</note> also teaches. After that, you must have good earth into which you wish to sow your <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurium*; here representing the spirit or volatile principle</note> and <term>Sun</term> <note>original: *Solem*; representing gold or the fixed soul principle</note>; it must be very well purified of all uncleanness, for without a purified earth, no seed can bear fruit. Therefore, the <term>Philosopher</term> <note>original: *Philosophus*; a general term for an alchemical authority</note> says: "Sow the seed in a fertile field that is prepared by the inextinguishable fire, and it will bring forth manifold fruit."

<note>original: *Vrina puerorum quid sit?*</note>

Right here, I wish to give you a true report of the <term>Urine of Boys</term> <note>original: *Vrina puerorum*; a common alchemical code name for a specific solvent or mineral essence, rarely referring to literal urine</note> and the <term>Urine of the Philosophers</term>: you should know that the spirit is drawn out of the metal, and that is the "urine of boys," for it is the <term>Seed of Metals</term> <note>original: *Sperma Metallorum*</note> and an origin of the metals. ## ->What is the Mercury of the Philosophers?<-

Mercury is nothing other than water and salt; these two elements have been <term>digested</term> <note>matured or processed through gentle heat</note> together for so long in natural heat that the two are united and <term>coagulated</term> <note>solidified from a liquid state</note>. When that has occurred, it is then called a "dry water" that is not wet and does not make things wet, and it is called Mercury. However, I am not speaking here of <term>raw mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurio crudo*; common quicksilver found in nature</note>, but of that Mercury through which the stars of the firmament work into the earth—a <term>substance</term> <note>original: *Corpus*</note> which is barely possible to grasp. For the Philosophers speak of the <term>Fifth Element</term> <note>original: *Quint-Element*</note>, which is the virtue of all growing things; this supreme power makes them ripe and perfect. The other and lowest <term>Quintessence</term> <note>original: *Quint Essenz*</note> is that which is in the earth and has partially become physical within it; for it enlarges the seed and multiplies that which it embraces according to its kind, and thus it grows out of the earth. In this way, the uppermost Quintessence comes to the aid of this lowest one; it clears away the coarseness of the earth and ripens the aforementioned seed, for it dries the "evil moisture" out of the Quintessence of the earth.

### Composition

<term>Albertus</term> <note>Albertus Magnus (c. 1200–1280), a medieval friar and philosopher credited with many alchemical works</note> speaks of the composition or joining of the stone thus: A quick or common joining cannot happen by way of coarse or solid things; instead, they must first be dissolved into a water, and then mixed with one another, and placed in a warm spot. Through natural heat, they unite in due time; for one must join the <term>Elixir</term>, then the two solutions together—taking three parts of the Elixir and one part of the broken <term>Corpus</term> <note>original: *Corpus*; the physical "body" or solid matter of the metal</note>. This coagulates and dissolves again; do this as often as necessary until it has become one single thing, without any further transformation. All this is accomplished through the goodness of our <term>mercurial water</term> <note>a philosophical solvent, not necessarily common liquid mercury</note>, for with it the body is dissolved; the water is cleansing, joining, dissolving, and makes the work both white and red. <margin><unclear>Aug</unclear></margin> <term>Aristotle</term> <note>The famous Greek philosopher to whom many medieval alchemical texts were apocryphally attributed</note> says <note>original: *inquit*</note> that this water is Mercury, and is the broken earth in which <term>Hermes</term> <note>Hermes Trismegistus</note> says to sow his seed—that is, the <term>Sol</term> <note>the Sun, representing Gold</note> or <term>Luna</term> <note>the Moon, representing Silver</note>. As <term>Senior</term> <note>Senior Zadith, a 10th-century alchemist often cited in medieval texts</note> speaks, they want to draw out the divine water of <term>Sulfur and Mercury</term> <note>original: *Sulphuris & Mercurii*; the two primary principles whose balance creates all metals</note> from the Sun and the Moon, which is fire,

## Third Treatise.
### Concerning the Assembly of the Philosophers.

1. Which I intend to interpret word for word, with all points and articles in particular, so that every reader may understand everything that the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: *Philosophi*; here referring specifically to alchemists, the "philosophers of nature"</note> write, so that he may proceed to the work more quickly and safely. Thereby praise God the Lord, and I wish for His grace at the beginning of my undertaking, to complete it, so that you, reader, may no longer be delayed, but may come to the true understanding of this our <term>Art</term> <note>original: *Kunst*; "The Art" is a standard historical term for alchemy</note>, just as these prepared Philosophers did. So you should now know that God the Almighty Himself first gave this Art to our father Adam in Paradise; for as soon as He had created him and placed him in Paradise, He gave him this Art, speaking thus: Adam, see, there are two things: that which is upwards is <term>volatile</term> <note>original: *flüchtig*; substances that evaporate or "fly" when heated</note>, and that which is downwards is <term>fixed</term> <note>original: *fix*; substances that remain solid and stable in the fire</note>, and in these two all secrets are hidden and enclosed. Keep this in great regard, and this secret power and virtue that is contained <note>original: *steckt*; this corresponds to the catchword for the next page</note>

## The Saying of *Abel*.

2. *Abel*, the son of *Adam*, wrote in his beginning that when God created our father *Adam* and placed him in Paradise, He cast all things—herbs, mountains, stones, and animals—that are not named, into our father *Adam*, so that he should be a lord over all things. From this, Nature caused man to be a mountain of all mountains, a stone of all stones, a tree of all trees, an herb of all herbs, a root of all roots, and an earth of all earths; and he may be named and regarded as such, because all the powers of all things are preserved within man; this, God gave to him.

Aristeus speaks in brief words thus: know that the <term>earthly realm</term> <note>original: *Erdreich*; refers to the element of Earth or the dry land.</note> is completely round, and not truly flat, for if the earth were level, the sun would shine everywhere and in all places in a single moment.

8. Pythagoras writes to us thus: note what that may be, which one feels and does not see, and does not recognize; that which one knows and does not see is heaven alone. And the earth is that which one does not recognize—that is in the world—along with the <term>integrity</term> <note>original: *Redlichkeit*; here used to mean the distinct, true quality or "rightness" of a sensory perception.</note> of the senses, such as seeing, hearing, smelling, tasting, and touching. In seeing, one recognizes the difference between white and black; in hearing, one recognizes the integrity of good and evil; likewise, in smelling, the good scent; in tasting, the bitterness from the sweetness; in touching, the coarseness and the <term>subtlety</term> <note>original: *Subtilheit*; in alchemy, this refers to the fineness or purity of a substance, as opposed to "gross" or impure matter.</note>.

9. Aristeus, in the second tablet, names the <term>body</term> <note>original: *Corpus*; the solid material being worked upon in the vessel.</note> that I have shown you, and makes it into thin leaves, and places upon it our <term>sea-water</term> <note>original: *Meerwasser*; a symbolic term for a potent alchemical solvent, likely a mercurial water.</note>, which is the <term>water of life</term> <note>original: *Wasser des Lebens*; also known as *aqua vitae*, a purified spirit or solvent used to animate the "dead" matter.</note>. He purifies it therewith in a gentle fire, and cooks it so long until its blackness vanishes through cooking, soaking, rubbing, and cleaning, until it becomes white, and then until it becomes red.

10. Parmenides: know that the <term>philosophers</term> <note>original: *Philosophi*; referring to the ancient alchemists and sages who guarded their secrets in riddles.</note> have spoken of many waters, of many stones and metals, so that they [would...] <note>The sentence ends abruptly due to the page break; the catchword *len* suggests the word *wollen* (want/wish).</note>

...[to] deceive themselves when seeking the Art. 11. **Lucas:** take the <term>living water</term> <note>A symbolic term for a potent alchemical solvent, often identified with mercury or a specialized "spirit" of the work.</note> of the moon, and <term>coagulate</term> <note>To thicken or solidify a liquid into a solid mass.</note> it according to our manner and custom. Do you not understand me when I say "according to our custom"? For it is already coagulated. I command you to take the living water that is from the moon and draw it onto our earth, until it becomes white; and this is our <term>magnesia</term> <note>In this context, not the modern magnesium oxide, but a name for the "First Matter" or the white stage of the alchemical work.</note>; thus the Nature of Natures rejoices.

12. **Ethel:** cook our stone until it shines like a gleaming <term>marble stone</term> <note>Refers to the desired appearance of the matter when it reaches the white, crystalline stage.</note>; then it becomes the great and secret stone. For <term>sulfur</term> <note>original: *Sulphur*; here representing the active, masculine, and "combustible" principle in matter.</note> is devoted to sulfur, keeping it within itself for the sake of its own property.

13. **Pythagoras:** we give you a rule for these things: that it drinks its own water, like the fire of the moon that you have prepared. 14. **Philetus** writes: know, all dear children of philosophy, the foundation—where many go astray—is nothing more than a thing of strong nature among the philosophers. 15. **Methuselah**: With the air, vapor, and spirit, we would hold common <term>mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurium*</note> in as high a value as <term>silver</term> <note>original: *Lunam*</note>, as it may or can exist through the nature of the minerals without heat.

...drawn from our Earth, so that they have meant the water of our fire, and fiery poison, extracted from all things and brought into our vinegar; however, though they speak of all things or of many things, it is yet nothing more than one thing, namely the lasting water. **Permanent water** <note>original: *Aqua permanens*; a stable alchemical solvent that does not evaporate</note>, and that is our vinegar.

30. **Bathon**: When the **matter** <note>original: *Materia*</note> of our stone is recognized, as well as the governance of the boiling and the colors that reveal themselves—as if he meant to say that it has as many names as it has colors—then in the first boiling it is called **putrefaction** <note>original: *putrifactio*; the stage where the matter decomposes and turns black</note>. Therein our stone is born, entirely black; and if you find it black, know then that the key to the Art is present and before your eyes, within which the white and red stand entirely hidden. Thus the philosophers say: it dissolves itself, it coagulates itself, it kills and becomes living again within itself, it makes itself black, white, and red, in Christian love and fundamental truth.

31. **Blodius**: Take the stone that is found in all places and is called **Rebis** <term>Rebis</term> <note>from the Latin *res bina*, meaning "double thing"; a symbol of the union of opposites, often depicted as a hermaphrodite</note>, and it grows in two mountains. Take it only while fresh, with its own blood; its increase is in the skin, also in the flesh, and its food is in the blood, and it dwells in the air. So take as much of it as you wish and bring it to the **bath** <note>original: *Bad*; likely referring to the *balneum Mariae* or water bath used for gentle heating</note>.

<summary>This page details the alchemical stages of putrefaction and the nature of the "Rebis." It emphasizes that despite many names and colors, the work involves a single "permanent water" and a self-transforming matter that must turn black before it can become white or red.</summary>

<keywords>Bathon, Blodius, Rebis, putrefaction, blackness, permanent water, alchemical bath, boiling, colors of the work</keywords>

32. **Lea the Prophetess** <note>original: *Weissagerin*</note> describes it to us with these brief words: You, Nathan, shall know that the flowers of gold are a stone; therefore, roast it for several days until it becomes like a shimmering marble stone.

33. **Alkius**: Every day you see the mountains standing before you, that is Man and Woman; so go to their pits, and establish and fetch their earths from there before they perish. <note>The "Man and Woman" represent the dual principles of alchemy—often Sulfur and Mercury—which the practitioner must extract from the raw "earth" of the material.</note>

34. **Bonellus**: All you followers of this Art, I say to you out of love and faithfulness: abandon the great labor of many different works, for our thing is but one thing, and it is called the living, permanent water. <note>original: *das lebendig bleibende Wasser* / *Aqua permanens*</note> Why? He who wanders among many words will eventually learn what he should guard himself against.

35. **Hieronymus**: The haters have disrupted our Art and falsified it with many words, poisoning the entire work. They speak of pennies of our earth, and of making Sun or Gold from our pennies, which they have named with many words. Thus, I now take this thing to salt, dissolve <note>original: *solviren*</note>, sublime <note>original: *sublimiren*</note>, wax, grind, turn to vinegar, turn into white **Sulfur** <term>Sulfur</term> <note>In alchemy, Sulfur represents the active, combustible, and masculine principle. "White sulfur" refers to a purified stage of the matter before it reaches the final "red" perfection.</note>, boil the fiery vapors safely and sufficiently, coagulate, and transform into red sulfur. All this, in appearance, is no more than one thing, just as it is first indestructible and incombustible in the white sulfur.

36. **Hermes**: It is the case that you the...

37. **Pythagoras**, in his fourth tablet:
How wonderfully wise is the fellowship of the Philosophers <note>original: *Philosophorum*</note> in their discernment, wherein they have spoken, first, that he has purified [the substance] from the very worst thing <note>original: *allerschlimmsten Dieß* (this thing). The "worst thing" refers to the *Prima Materia*, the base, unrefined matter that looks worthless to the ignorant.</note> into the most noble. And if it were the case that the common people recognized and knew the "very worst thing"—which is so small and unhonored—they would take it for a great lie. Yet, if they knew the power or strength of this "worst thing," they would not despise the noble substance. For this reason, our Lord God has suppressed and hidden it from them, so that haters or unworthy, godless scoundrels might not recognize it and thereby strengthen their own malice.

38. **Hagienus**: Our <term>Stone</term> <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold and granting longevity.</note> is compared to all trees, all herbs, and animals; it is found within all people, is of many colors, contains the four elements within itself, and is called the **Microcosm** <term>Minor Mundus</term> <note>Latin for "Lesser World." This is the philosophical concept that the human being (or the Stone) contains all the elements and qualities of the entire universe (the Macrocosm).</note>. Now rejoice, you inexperienced ones—you who try to perform the "tinting" <note>The process of transmuting or "coloring" a base metal to turn it into gold.</note> with [just any] stone, with an earth and **Sulfur** <term>Sulphur</term> <note>The active, combustible principle in alchemy.</note>, with **Alum** <term>Allaun</term> <note>A chemical compound used as a mordant or astringent.</note>, **Borax** <term>Borax</term>, lime, **Saltpeter** <term>Salpeter</term> <note>Potassium nitrate, often used in making nitric acid.</note>, and the like; you who search every day with your "truth" and your seeking, yet find nothing. Do you not see the mountains lying before your eyes every day, from which you might obtain or acquire the stones? Now rejoice, for I will show you the place where you shall find everything; the first is found in the heights...

<meta>This page continues the list of authoritative alchemical testimonies begun on the previous page, specifically concluding the thoughts attributed to Rosinus and moving into the views of Bernhard of Trevisan, Geber, Aros, and Arnold of Villanova regarding the "mercurial" foundation of the Stone.</meta>

<page-num>99</page-num>
<header>✽  💮  ✽  99</header>
<sig>G 2</sig>

<insert>Index of signs</insert> <note>original: "zeÿg Verzeichniß"</note>

...is tempered together from the dry and the moist; and Mercury <note>original: *Mercurius*; in alchemy, this is not just the liquid metal, but the "spirit" or "principle" of liquidity and transformation</note> is both dry and moist in its nature, and all things have their growth from the dry and the moist.

### Count Bernhard <note>Bernhard Trevisan (1406–1490), a famous Italian alchemist who spent his life and fortune seeking the Philosopher's Stone.</note>

57. **Geber** <note>The Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan, an 8th-century polymath; his works formed the backbone of medieval European alchemy.</note>: we have not been able to find any thing that remains and holds fast in the fire, except alone the **viscous moisture** <note>original: *viscous humiditas*</note>, the natural moisture, which is the root of all metals. For the worthy stone, one needs nothing more than the mercurial substances, well-purified by Art, penetrating, coloring, persisting in the fire, standing in the battle of the fire, not abandoning themselves in the kingdoms <note>original: *Monarchien*; here referring to the chemical stages or "regimes" of the process</note>, but always maintaining themselves in their mercurial essence; that is a thing which unites itself with the metal at the foundation of the root, and breaks their imperfect form, and leads them into another form, according to the power of the Elixir or **Medicine** <note>original: *Medicina*</note>.

58. **Aros** <note>A figure often cited in the *Turba Philosophorum*, sometimes associated with the legendary Maria the Prophetess.</note>: our medicine is made from two things of one essence, from the mercurial unity, fixed and non-fixed, spiritual, bodily, cold and moist, hot and dry, and from other things it cannot be made.

59. **Arnoldus** <note>Arnold of Villanova (c. 1240–1311), a Catalan physician and alchemist.</note>: let your entire intention be to cook and to digest the mercurial substance, and according to its worthiness, it will dignify the bodies <note>original: *Corpora*; the solid metallic materials being worked upon</note>.

<summary>This page presents a collection of axioms from famous alchemical authorities—Geber, Count Bernhard, Aros, and Arnold of Villanova—emphasizing that "mercury" (the principle of moisture and fluidity) is the essential root of the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>

<keywords>Mercury, Geber, Bernhard of Trevisan, viscous moisture, Elixir, Arnold of Villanova, Aros, alchemy, metallic root</keywords>

60. **Alphidius** <note>An authority frequently cited in medieval alchemical compendiums, likely representing an earlier Arabic source translated into Latin.</note>: Transform nature, and you will find what you seek; this is true, for in our mastery <note>The "Mastery" or *Magisterium* refers to the successful completion of the alchemical process.</note> we make the delicate from the coarse, that is, the spirit from the corpse; thereafter, the dry from the moist, that is, the earth from the water. And thus we transform and create the true nature, making the physical spiritual and the spiritual physical; as it is said, the lowest is turned upward and the highest downward—that is, the spirit becomes a corpse, and the corpse becomes a spirit.

61. **Bernhardus** <note>Bernhard Trevisan, as mentioned on the previous page.</note>: The means are nothing other than congealed <term>Mercurius</term> <note>Mercury; in this context, the "Philosophical Mercury" which acts as the universal solvent.</note>, and the <term>Prima materia</term> <note>First Matter; the original, undifferentiated substance required to start the Work.</note> is a double <term>Mercurius</term>. For our <term>Medicin</term> <note>Medicine; another name for the Philosopher's Stone in its healing capacity for both metals and humans.</note> is made of two things: fixed and unfixed, spiritual and physical, cold and moist, hot and dry. Likewise, the <term>Mercurius</term> shall be cooked in a threefold vessel <margin>The raw heat of the fire, the fire of the earth, and the light fire <note>These refer to the "degrees" of heat used in the furnace, from gentle to intense.</note></margin>, <note>original Latin: *ut siccitas ignis agentis, convertat in vaporosam humiditatem, olei circumdantis materiam und ignis non digerit materiam nostram, sed calor ejus alteratus in siccitatem*</note> so that the dryness of the acting fire transforms the matter into a vaporous moisture of the oil surrounding the substance, and the fire does not consume our matter, but its heat is altered into dryness, which is the true fire.

<margin>unpleasant vapors are percei[ved] <unclear>...</unclear> one [smells] the honey correctly <note>A common alchemical observation: the transition from foul-smelling "blackness" to a sweet-smelling stage.</note></margin>
62. **Stephanus** <note>Stephanus of Alexandria (c. 7th century), a philosopher and alchemist whose work linked Neoplatonism with chemical practice.</note>, *on metals* <note>original: *de metallis*</note>: Metals are earthly bodies and are born in water; the water draws the vapor out from the stone, and from the moisture of the earth, through the action of the sun, God the Lord lets the gold grow, and [it is] together <insert>cooked; and the fire below is not introduced into the matter itself, but rather it is a secret fire in all beings <unclear>...</unclear> <unclear>...</unclear> <unclear>...</unclear> <unclear>...</unclear></insert>

but rather, thus Earth and Water are united into a single metallic body.

63. **Guido Bonatus** <note>Guido Bonatti (c. 1210–1296) was the most famous Italian astrologer of the 13th century, also cited in alchemical texts regarding the influence of the heavens on matter.</note> writes briefly of the <term>Quint Essenz</term> <note>Quintessence; literally the "fifth element," a celestial substance thought to be more refined than the four earthly elements (earth, air, fire, water).</note> thus: that it is the very purest of the four elements—that is, the <term>Quint Essenz</term>, which encloses all four elements within itself. This is the <term>Prima materia</term> <note>First Matter; the primordial substance from which all things are made.</note>, from which God created all things and still creates daily. And this "confused mass" <note>original Latin: *massa confusa*; a term for the chaotic state of matter before it is organized by nature or the alchemist.</note> (Folio 37) is the nature of every thing, whatever it may be; yet it becomes one nature in all things and is called <term>Hyle</term> <note>A Greek term meaning "matter" or "substance," often used in alchemy to denote the raw, unformed state of the universe.</note>, as I have made mention above.

64. **Alridos** <note>Likely a variation of Alphidius or another medieval authority.</note>: For every thing, whatever it may be on earth, its virtue, power, and magnificent effect lie hidden in its <term>Quint Essenz</term>, whether it be of a warm, cold, moist, or dry nature and temperament <note>The "complexion" or balance of the four humors/qualities.</note>. And within the <term>Quint Essenz</term> of every thing, the most excellent and noble scent and taste is required that exists, or may exist, or can be imagined; for herein the highest perfection is necessary.

65. **Longinus** <note>A name likely attributed to a legendary or historical figure, common in alchemical pseudepigrapha.</note> describes the Work to us thus: take care that your vessel is constantly sealed and maintains a steady heat. He says further: this water is prepared in dry ashes <note>Referring to an ash-bath (*balneum cinere*), which provided a gentle, even heat for the glass vessel.</note> within a sealed vessel; it must be boiled until two become one, and when they are mixed into this one, then the corpse <note>The solid, "dead" matter at the bottom of the vessel.</note> is turned into spirit.

In nature, Mercury is cold and moist, while <term>Sulfur</term> <note>original: *Sulphur*; representing the active, masculine principle in alchemy.</note> is hot and dry, and both are brought forth in nature within a single thing.

# Now follows the true interpretation of several sayings of the philosophers set forth earlier, word for word, regarding all points and articles of their opinions.

So that you, reader, may understand more successfully what the <term>philosophers</term> <note>In this context, "philosophers" refers specifically to true alchemists who possess the secret knowledge of the Great Work, rather than academic or moral philosophers.</note> meant with their secret, hidden sayings—for all the aforementioned were true and righteous philosophers who knew and practiced this <term>Art</term> <note>original: *Kunst*; a standard term for the "Great Work" of alchemy.</note>—and although there have been many more of them since the time of <term>Adam</term> <note>Alchemists often traced their lineage back to Adam, believing the knowledge was a divine gift given to the first man.</note>, not all of them have come into my hands. It is also impossible to recount every one here, since the foundation and truth are sufficiently and abundantly taught and demonstrated in those mentioned above; indeed, even the least among them would suffice. 

However, that I have named so many here, gathered them, and introduced them to one another was done for your benefit, so that you, reader, may be better taught and instructed, and may work with greater certainty, and also that you may know how to guard yourself against other wasteful and useless <term>expenses of money</term> <note>original: *Geldköstungen*; a common warning against "puffers"—fraudulent or ignorant alchemists who bankrupted themselves and their patrons using incorrect chemical processes.</note>. Therefore, out of Christian love, I have signified and interpreted these true philosophers according to my understanding, as much as God has granted me, and I ask everyone experienced in this Art, who knows and understands it, also to [work] just as I have worked,

...expressed, so that he <note>the reader</note> would not despise me if I had perhaps erred in words while writing, but would instead wish to improve what is wrong out of a faithful heart and mind, just as I have done, and lead no one wickedly astray or deceive them, so that God’s name may be honored and the neighbor’s benefit improved. However, you should not change my sense and meaning, for I know that what I have written is the divine truth.

## The interpretation of the Saying of Adam follows thus.

When God the Almighty created our first father <term>Adam</term> <note>Alchemists often claimed their knowledge was "Primal Wisdom" given to Adam by God in Eden, then passed down through the Patriarchs.</note> and placed him in Paradise, He showed him two things: Earth and Water. The Earth is <term>fixed</term> <note>original: *fix*; in alchemical terms, a "fixed" substance is stable and does not evaporate or change when exposed to heat.</note> and stable, while the Water is <term>volatile</term> <note>original: *flüchtig*; "volatile" substances are those that easily evaporate or escape the crucible during heating.</note> and unstable; and within these two are hidden all manner of things that God has created.

Thus, Air dwells within Water, and within the Earth—by the creation and order of God—dwell stones and Fire. From these <term>four elements</term> <note>The traditional building blocks of the universe: Earth, Water, Air, and Fire.</note> all things in heaven and on earth spring forth through God's creation and order. Just as Fire, stones, ore, salt, <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurius*; here referring to "Philosophical Mercury," the fluid principle that alchemists believed was the root of all metals.</note>, and all kinds of metals and minerals dwell within the Earth; so too on the earth, in the water, and in the air, dwell all kinds of living, sentient, growing, and essential things, such as animals,

Birds, fish, flesh and blood, bones, wood, trees, flowers, leaves, and the like—whatever they may be—and to all these things God has given to each its own <term>virtue</term> <note>original: *Tugend*; in early modern natural philosophy, this refers to the inherent medicinal, spiritual, or physical properties of a substance.</note>, power, and <term>operation</term> <note>original: *Würkung*; the specific way a substance acts upon the body or in a chemical reaction.</note>. He has subjected all these things to Adam and set him as a lord over them, so that he should use all these things for the sustenance and health of his body, and should feed, preserve, and strengthen his body with them. In these very things, it is clearly found that all things harmonize with human nature, since man takes the power of things into himself through the intake of food, which must then unite and agree with man's nature. From this, you may well recognize that the <term>knowledge</term> <note>original: *Erkåntniß*.</note> of all things is hidden within man, according to wisdom, understanding, and reason.

That this is so, note what is written in the Book of Moses, Genesis 1: in the first year of the world, on the 6th day—which was the 15th day of March <note>original: *Martii*. Early modern chronologists often attempted to calculate the exact date of Creation; March was frequently chosen because it marks the spring equinox.</note>—the Lord God created the first man, Adam, from the lineage of <term>red earth</term> <note>original: *rothen Erden*; this is a play on the Hebrew name "Adam," which is related to *adama* (earth/ground) and *adom* (red). ## The Interpretation of the Saying of **Abel**.

This saying almost explains itself, and it is also sufficiently reported in the words of God and the saying of **Adam**. But according to my opinion and knowledge, I say this about it: that man has within himself all the powers of those things that this saying clearly brings with it—that man is accounted to be a **Microcosm** <note>original: "kleine Welt"; the philosophical concept that the human being is a miniature map or reflection of the entire universe (the Macrocosm).</note>, and he compares himself entirely to the world. For his bones in the flesh are compared to stones and mountains, with which the **body** <note>original: "Corpus"; refers here to both the human physical body and the "body" of a material substance in alchemy.</note> is fortified, just as the earth with

...with stones, and the flesh is accounted for the **earth**, and the large veins for the large **rivers** <note>original: "Revier"; an archaic term for watercourses or districts, here referring to rivers or streams.</note> or waters, the small veins for the small rivers that flow into the large ones; the bladder is the **sea**, wherein the large rivers gather with the small; the hair to the growing **herbs**, and the nails on hands and feet—and whatever is on the outside of man, each according to its kind—are compared to the world.

The internal parts of man—heart, lungs, and liver, and the like—are also compared to the **metals** in the mountains. But this hair, these are the roots, and they have their roots in the air and their head in the earth—that is, in the flesh—wherein they have their power and growth. As the **Philosophers** <note>Refers to the alchemists, often called "Philosophers of Nature."</note> also say: the root of their **minerals** is in the air, and their head is in the earth; and his root, as his feet, is in the air. For that which rises up through **distillation** <term>Distillation: a process of heating a substance to create vapor and then condensing it back into liquid to purify it or separate components.</term> is **volatile** <note>original: "flüchtig"; refers to a substance that easily evaporates or escapes into the air.</note>; in that, he has his feet in the air. And that which remains below at the bottom is **fixed** <note>original: "fix"; refers to a substance that is stable and resists the heat of the fire.</note>; that is his head in the earth. For the feet carry the head, and the head governs the feet; therefore, one cannot exist without the other if it is to have any effect.

For this reason, man is compared to an **inverted tree** <note>original: "verkehrten Baum"; the *Arbor Inversa* is a mystical concept where man's spiritual "roots" are in the heavens (air) and his physical body grows downward.</note>, for he has his roots—the hair—in the air, while other trees have their hair—that is, the roots—in the earth. And in the same way, it is also structured with our **Stone** <note>The "Philosopher's Stone," the ultimate goal of alchemy.</note>; therefore may...

The **Philosophers** <note>Refers to alchemists, the "Philosophers of Nature."</note> say well: they have their head in the earth and the root in the air. You may also well take a comparison from a fountain; for the water that flows rushing represents the root of its minerals, and the essence <note>original: "Saft"; literally "juice" or "sap," referring here to the vital liquid or spirit.</note> of the water is the head and is in the earth. And this comparison gives two kinds of understanding: first, where the **material** <note>original: "Materia"; the substance used to begin the Great Work.</note> of our Stone is found; and second, it gives to understand the separation and rejoining of the Stone. For when our Stone has risen through the **still-head** <term>Helm: the upper part of a distilling apparatus (alembic) where vapors gather and condense.</term> into the **receiver** <term>Vorlag: the vessel placed at the end of the still to collect the condensed liquid.</term>, it then has its root in the air; if it is now to reach its power and root, it must be brought back into its earth. Then it has its head in the earth and possesses its perfect power. Thus, according to these sayings, man—reckoned as a **microcosm** <note>original: "kleine Welt"; literally "small world," the idea that man mirrors the entire universe.</note>—may also rightly be held as such; for he is a mountain of all mountains from which we recognize our ore, according to understanding. For our work is accounted as a small world, according to how it develops, just as the world was created; for that is the **First Matter** <term>Prima Materia: the original, formless substance from which all things were created and which alchemists sought to isolate.</term>, an origin of all things, from which every new thing is prepared. For you should know what the **First Matter** is: something buried everywhere in the earth that decays or digests itself, just as food is digested in man; and in this decay <note>original: "Faulen"; referring to putrefaction, the stage of breaking down a substance to release its essence.</note>, the coarse separates from the **subtle** <term>Subtilen: the refined, pure, or spiritual essence of a substance, as opposed to its dense, physical form.</term>, as the stench from the pure, and the pure is

## Interpretation of the Saying of Isindri.

Oh God! In what a wondrous manner the **Philosophers** <note>The "Philosophers" refers to the ancient and medieval alchemical authorities (the Adepts).</note> have hidden this thing! But the Philosophers write truly and rightly; yet who can understand and grasp it, if one has no foundation in these matters? Truly, no one can; and even if you were already a **Doctor of Doctors** <note>original: "Doctor Doctorum"; a title for a preeminent teacher or scholar.</note> and a **Light of the World** <note>original: "Lux Mundi"; a Latin title implying supreme intellectual or spiritual enlightenment.</note>, you would still be entirely blind to this.
